  • Relevance to HIVE: Copy Flower's framework-agnostic, federated orchestration model — a central SuperLink coordinating distributed SuperNode agents with compute moving to the data — and its Apache-2.0 open core plus paid enterprise platform monetization; avoid relying on a single central data pool when privacy or distributed ownership matters.
  • Sources: https://flower.ai/; https://flower.ai/blog/2026-02-06-announcing-flower-1.26-release; https://preview.flower.ai/blog/2024-02-15-announcing-series-a; https://techcrunch.com/2023/08/08/flower-lands-3-6m-to-grow-its-platform-for-federated-learning/

88. NVIDIA Triton / NIM multi-model inference — In Progress

  • Category: Orchestration/Enterprise
  • What it is: NVIDIA's open-source, backend-agnostic inference server (Triton, now Dynamo-Triton) that serves many models from different frameworks concurrently behind one HTTP/gRPC API, packaged per-model as NIM containerized microservices.
  • Key dates: Triton repo created 2018-10-04 (as TensorRT Inference Server, renamed Triton in 2020); NVIDIA NIM launched 18 Mar 2024 at GTC; Triton folded into NVIDIA Dynamo as Dynamo-Triton in Mar 2025; latest verified release v2.75.0 / container 26.11, Nov 2026.
  • Evidence: GitHub: 11,153 stars, 1,874 forks, 3,904 commits, 97 releases; current release v2.75.0 / NGC container 26.11 in November 2026, with roughly monthly releases (v2.74.0 on 2 Nov 2026, v2.72.0 in Aug 2026). NIM catalog reported at 100+ models by May 2026, with NVIDIA claiming NIM 1.4 delivered 2.4x faster inference (vendor benchmark, not independently audited). Production users cited include Amazon, Microsoft, Oracle Cloud, DocuSign, Perplexity and Snap.
  • Why: It succeeded as infrastructure rather than as an agent framework: it is hardware-coupled to NVIDIA's dominant GPU installed base, backend-agnostic (TensorRT, PyTorch, ONNX, vLLM and others), and maintained on a monthly release cadence inside NVIDIA AI Enterprise. Rather than being abandoned, it was absorbed upward into Dynamo in 2025 for large-scale LLM serving, while Triton remains the general multi-model layer — a sign of sustained adoption, though single-LLM workloads increasingly prefer dedicated engines such as vLLM or SGLang.
  • Relevance to HIVE: Copy Triton/NIM's backend-agnostic, one-API serving layer with concurrent multi-model execution, ensembles and dynamic batching — but avoid locking HIVE to a single LLM engine or GPU vendor's packaging.
  • Sources: https://github.com/triton-inference-server/server; https://www.globenewswire.com/en/news-release/2024/03/18/2848210/0/en/NVIDIA-Launches-Generative-AI-Microservices-for-Developers-to-Create-and-Deploy-Generative-AI-Copilots-Across-NVIDIA-CUDA-GPU-Installed-Base.html; https://docs.nvidia.com/deeplearning/triton-inference-server/release-notes/rel-26-08.html; https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/?p=122739

89. vLLM multi-model serving — Succeeded

  • Category: Orchestration/Enterprise
  • What it is: vLLM is an open-source, high-throughput LLM inference and serving engine built on PagedAttention and continuous batching, used to serve many models in production via separate per-model server instances behind a router or production stack.
  • Key dates: Repository created 2023-02-09; PagedAttention paper at SOSP 2023; joined the PyTorch Foundation as a hosted project on 7 May 2025; v0.31.0 released 5 October 2026.
  • Evidence: GitHub (Nov 2026): ~97,184 stars, 24,989 forks, 25,749 commits, 110 releases, 2,000+ contributors, Apache-2.0; production users include Amazon, LinkedIn, Meta, Mistral, Roblox and Stripe; creators' company Inferact raised a $150M seed round in January 2026 at an $800M valuation. Important nuance: a single vLLM server process does not support multiple models — multi-model serving is done with separate instances on different ports/GPUs behind a router/load balancer, or via the vLLM Production Stack / llm-d; it does support efficient multi-LoRA serving on one base model.
  • Why: It succeeded technically by solving KV-cache memory waste with PagedAttention and continuous batching, delivering high throughput for many concurrent requests, and by covering 200+ model architectures across NVIDIA, AMD, TPU and other hardware. Adoption compounded through open-source neutrality under the PyTorch Foundation, an OpenAI-compatible API, and production validation at hyperscale users, which made it the default serving layer rather than a niche engine.
  • Relevance to HIVE: Copy vLLM's pattern of one specialised engine per model plus a router/production-stack for multi-model orchestration, and avoid cramming multiple full models into a single process.
  • Sources: https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm; https://docs.vllm.ai/en/latest/; https://discuss.vllm.ai/t/run-multiple-models/1181/2; https://fish.audio/blog/open-source-llm-inference-engines-2026/

90. Palantir AIP (Artificial Intelligence Platform) — Succeeded

  • Category: Orchestration/Enterprise
  • What it is: Palantir's enterprise generative-AI platform that connects LLMs and AI agents to an organization's live data, workflows and operations through its Ontology, with governance, evals and human oversight.
  • Key dates: Palantir founded 2003; AIP launched April 2023 (began deploying in 2023); still active through 2025-2026, no shutdown or pivot.
  • Evidence: Palantir FY2025 revenue $4.475B (+56% YoY), with U.S. commercial revenue $1.5B (+109%); total customers 1,007 as of Q1 2026 (up 31% YoY), U.S. commercial customers 571+ at end-2025; Q1 2026 revenue $1.633B (+85% YoY); 850+ cumulative AIP Bootcamp trials by end-Q1 2026 with ~38% converting to paid contracts within 90 days; net dollar retention 134-139%. AIP is actively documented in 2026 with a 12-layer agentic architecture, AIP Logic, AIP Chatbot Studio and AIP Evals.
  • Why: It succeeded commercially because it solved the enterprise adoption bottleneck — grounding LLMs in governed operational data and actions rather than offering a standalone chatbot — and paired that with intensive five-day AIP Bootcamps that produced working prototypes on customers' own data. Timing also favoured it: launched in April 2023 at the peak of enterprise LLM demand, it rode Palantir's existing Foundry/Gotham footprint and government credibility into rapid commercial expansion. The trade-off is cost and complexity: it is a heavyweight, expensive platform requiring deep deployment engagement, so it wins large enterprises and government, not small teams.
  • Relevance to HIVE: Copy AIP's Ontology-first grounding — agents act only on a governed, permissioned model of data, decisions and actions with audit trails and evals — and avoid its heavyweight, high-touch bootcamp deployment model.
  • Sources: https://www.palantir.com/docs/foundry/aip/overview; https://investors.palantir.com/files/2025%20FY%20PLTR%2010-K.pdf; http://fourweekmba.com/palantir-revenue-breakdown/?utm_source=rss&utm_medium=rss&utm_campaign=palantir-revenue-breakdown

91. ServiceNow Now Assist / agent mesh (AI Agent Orchestrator + AI Agent Fabric, now ServiceNow Otto) — In Progress

  • Category: Orchestration/Enterprise
  • What it is: ServiceNow's generative-AI layer on the Now Platform — Now Assist skills plus multi-agent AI Agents coordinated by AI Agent Orchestrator and connected to external agents via AI Agent Fabric (MCP/A2A) — now unified under the ServiceNow Otto experience.
  • Key dates: Now Assist launched Sept 2023 (Vancouver release, GA Sept 29, 2023); AI Agents and AI Agent Orchestrator announced Jan 29, 2025, available Mar 2025; Moveworks acquired for $2.85B, closed Dec 15, 2025; AI Agent Fabric external-agent (A2A) support GA Dec 2025; EmployeeWorks launched Feb 26, 2026; Otto announced May 5, 2026 at Knowledge 2026, folding Now Assist, Moveworks and AI Experience into one experience; legacy Pro Plus/Enterprise Plus tiers ended sale July 1, 2026, replaced by Foundation/Advanced/Prime.
  • Evidence: Now Assist reached ~$600M ACV in Q4 2025 (up from ~$250M in Aug 2025, target >$1B by end of 2026), with deals more than tripling YoY and Now Assist deals including 5+ products up 10x YoY; reported 1,000+ enterprise customers adopted Now Assist with Pro Plus SKU growing 40%+ YoY; ServiceNow serves 85%+ of the Fortune 500 (8,800 customers end-2025). Counterpoint: independent analysis put Now Assist attach under 30% of the installed base in early 2025, with AI seats reportedly bought at ~2x realistic year-one usage.
  • Why: It succeeded commercially because it rides an entrenched workflow platform and CMDB — agents act on real enterprise data and existing ITSM/HR/SecOps workflows rather than starting from scratch — but adoption is slower than the vendor narrative because the value is bundled into expensive tier upgrades (Pro Plus/Enterprise Plus, now Prime), consumption is metered in 'assists' (agents cost 25–150 assists per run vs 1 for a skill), and ROI proof outside large reference customers remains thin. The May 2026 Otto rebrand also signals the standalone Now Assist brand was not enough on its own; ServiceNow had to buy Moveworks ($2.85B) and merge three AI surfaces to deliver a unified front door.
  • Relevance to HIVE: Copy the pattern of grounding agents in a shared system of record and a central orchestrator with a governance control tower, and price/meter agent runs by outcome cost — but avoid bundling agents behind premium tiers and opaque consumption metering that slows adoption.
  • Sources: https://s205.q4cdn.com/537566246/files/doc_news/ServiceNow-Otto-creates-the-unified-AI-experience-for-the-enterprise-2026.pdf; https://www.servicenow.com/workflow/platform-foundations/unified-ai-platform-ready-anything.html; https://harmony.io/insights/servicenow-ai-agents; https://www.datatobrief.com/blog/ai-agent-stocks-autonomous-software-investing

92. Adept AI (ACT-1 / Fuyu) — Stalled/Abandoned

  • Category: Orchestration/Enterprise
  • What it is: Adept AI built ACT-1, an Action Transformer that observes screens and operates software via clicks and keystrokes, and the Fuyu family of multimodal models optimized for UI understanding.
  • Key dates: Founded/emerged from stealth Apr 26, 2022; ACT-1 demo Sep 14, 2022; Series B Mar 14, 2023; Fuyu-8B Oct 17, 2023; Fuyu-Heavy Jan 24, 2024; Amazon reverse acqui-hire/licensing deal Jun 28, 2024; last public blog post Aug 23, 2024; no formal shutdown announced
  • Evidence: Raised $65M Series A (Apr 2022) and $350M Series B at a ~$1B valuation (Mar 2023), $415M total. ACT-1 demoed Sep 2022 but stayed in beta for ~2 years with no widely available commercial product; Fuyu-8B open-sourced Oct 2023 (~11k Hugging Face downloads/month still cited in 2026) and Fuyu-Heavy announced Jan 2024. On Jun 28, 2024 Amazon hired CEO David Luan and co-founders Augustus Odena, Maxwell Nye, Erich Elsen and Kelsey Szot plus other staff and licensed Adept's agent tech/models/datasets (~$25M reported license payment); ~20 employees remained under new CEO Zach Brock, pivoting to enterprise agentic solutions. FTC probed the deal in Jul 2024; Adept's last blog post was Aug 23, 2024, its pricing page 404s by 2026, and no new funding, release or shutdown has been verified since — while Luan left Amazon in Feb 2026 and Amazon closed its SF AGI lab in Jul 2026.
  • Why: Adept tried to train frontier foundation models and build an enterprise agent product simultaneously, but ACT-1 never reached product-market fit and stayed in beta for two years while OpenAI, Anthropic and Google shipped competing computer-use capabilities. The economics were unsustainable — Luan himself said frontier agent work needed multi-billion-dollar compute clusters — so in June 2024 the company effectively surrendered its mission in a reverse acqui-hire, with Amazon licensing the tech and hiring the founders, leaving a ~20-person rump with no meaningful public release or news since August 2024.
  • Relevance to HIVE: Copy Fuyu's radically simple encoder-free screen-grounding for computer-use agents, but avoid Adept's fatal split — never fund a frontier foundation model and an enterprise product at the same time without a shipped, paying vertical first.
  • Sources: https://aiwiki.ai/wiki/adept_ai; https://www.anygen.io/showcase/adept-ai/index.html; https://rottenwifi.com/amazon-hired-adepts-founders-in-2024-what-happened-to-the-ai-startup-deal/; https://fundraisingfox.com/companies/adept-4

93. Rabbit R1 / Large Action Model — In Progress

  • Category: Cognitive architecture/AGI
  • What it is: Rabbit R1 is a $199 handheld AI device running rabbitOS (based on Android) built around Rabbit's Large Action Model (LAM), pitched as an AI that operates apps and websites on the user's behalf instead of just answering questions.
  • Key dates: Founded as Rabbit Inc. (formerly Cyber Manufacturing Co.) by Jesse Lyu; R1 announced 9 Jan 2024 at CES 2024, shipped spring 2024; LAM Playground Oct 2024, teach mode Nov 2024; rabbitOS 2 on 8 Sept 2025; OS3 general availability 22 Sept 2026; R1 manufacturing stopped Sept 2026, no R2 planned.
  • Evidence: Announced 9 Jan 2024 at CES at $199 with no subscription; 100,000-130,000 units sold (company claims 100,000+ delivered, <5% returns, 45-50% hardware margin, ~$60M raised total, 15 employees — self-reported, not independently verified); only ~5,000 of ~100,000 buyers were active at any moment in Sept 2024 (The Verge, citing CEO Jesse Lyu); raised $20M in Oct 2023 and $10M in Dec 2023 from Khosla Ventures and others; rabbitOS 2 shipped 8 Sept 2025 and OS3 reached general availability 22 Sept 2026, but WIRED reported manufacturing of the R1 had stopped, stock was dwindling, and there are no plans for an R2 — the pivot is to OS3 software on users' own computers plus a planned Cyberdeck vibe-coding device. Reports in 2025 of unpaid staff and an Oct 2025 Chapter 11 filing for Tech Rabbit Inc. are third-party/unverified in the sources checked.
  • Why: The CES demo outran the shipping product: at launch it had only a handful of integrations, reviewers gave it 3/10, and investigations showed the 'LAM' largely relied on cloud-operated Android VMs and scripts rather than a learned general action model, so retention collapsed to ~5% of buyers. Economics and market timing compounded this — a single-purpose $199 gadget could not justify itself against smartphones that gained the same AI features for free — and after reported financial distress in 2025 Rabbit pivoted from R1 hardware to OS3, cross-device agent software running on computers users already own.
  • Relevance to HIVE: Copy Rabbit's teach-mode idea of learning actions from demonstrated workflows, but avoid demo-first dedicated hardware and general-agent claims — ship the agent as software on devices users already own and prove reliability on narrow tasks first.
  • Sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rabbit_r1; https://www.wired.com/story/rabbit-r1-os3-jesse-lyu/; https://heytalaya.com/2026/10/humane-ai-pin-rabbit-r1-what-happened/

94. Humane AI Pin — Failed

  • Category: Orchestration/Enterprise
  • What it is: A screenless, voice-controlled wearable AI pin intended as a smartphone replacement, powered by Humane's cloud-based CosmOS platform.
  • Key dates: Founded 2018; AI Pin unveiled 9 Nov 2023; shipped Apr 2024; HP asset deal announced 18 Feb 2025; AI Pin discontinued and servers shut down 28 Feb 2025
  • Evidence: Raised about $230M; launched at $699 plus $24/month, cut to $499 in Oct 2024; only about 10,000 units shipped (~7,000 still in customers' hands, ~$9M revenue) with returns at times outpacing sales; HP acquired CosmOS, 300+ patents/applications and most staff for $116M on 18 Feb 2025, and Humane shut down AI Pin servers at noon PT on 28 Feb 2025, bricking core calling, messaging and AI functions.
  • Why: Reviews found it slow, inaccurate, hot, short on battery, and with a laser display unreadable in sunlight, while its $699 price plus $24/month subscription offered no compelling advantage over a phone. Poor adoption and returns, compounded by a charging-case fire-risk warning, made the economics unsustainable, and because its intelligence lived in the cloud, the Feb 2025 shutdown left sold devices effectively bricked — HP bought the platform, patents and team, not the consumer product.
  • Relevance to HIVE: Avoid HIVE becoming a cloud-dependent, premium-priced single-purpose product with no local fallback — prove a clear use case users cannot get from a phone, and design graceful degradation so a shutdown or service change never bricks the system.
  • Sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humane_Inc.; https://www.hp.com/us-en/newsroom/press-releases/2025/hp-accelerates-ai-software-investments-to-transform-the-future-of-work.html; https://techcrunch.com/2026/11/14/the-ai-graveyard-a-running-list-of-projects-and-startups-that-didnt-make-it/

95. Microsoft AutoGen Studio / Magentic-UI — Failed

  • Category: Multi-agent framework
  • What it is: Microsoft's open-source multi-agent framework with a no-code Studio for building agent teams, and its human-centred web-agent prototype Magentic-UI (later MagenticLite) built on AutoGen/Magentic-One.
  • Key dates: AutoGen launched Sep 2023; AutoGen Studio introduced 17 Jun 2024; AutoGen v0.4 released 19 Feb 2025; Magentic-UI launched 19 May 2025 (repo created 5 May 2025); AutoGen v0.7.5, last release, 30 Sep 2025; maintenance mode Oct 2025; Microsoft Agent Framework 1.0 GA 2 Apr 2026; Magentic-UI 0.2/MagenticLite May 2026, last main commit 23 Jul 2026; AutoGen archived Nov 2026, with the AI Frontiers group behind it dissolved/reorganised in Sep-Nov 2026.
  • Evidence: AutoGen reached ~61,805 GitHub stars but its last release was v0.7.5 on 30 Sep 2025; Microsoft put it in maintenance mode in Oct 2025 and by Nov 2026 its repo was archived with no bug/security fixes, directing users to Microsoft Agent Framework 1.0 (GA 2 Apr 2026). Magentic-UI reached ~10,117 stars and 820 commits; on GAIA it scored 30.3% autonomous, rising to 51.9% with a simulated user with side information, but its 0.2 MagenticLite pivot in May 2026 broke compatibility with 0.1 and its last main-branch commit was 23 Jul 2026 (PyPI 0.2.2, 21 Jul 2026).
  • Why: AutoGen was a research prototype that never reached 1.0 and was explicitly not production-ready, so Microsoft consolidated it with Semantic Kernel into the enterprise-supported Microsoft Agent Framework instead of sustaining two overlapping stacks. Magentic-UI compounded this by pivoting from frontier-model 0.1 to small-model MagenticLite 0.2 with no database migration, while its parent research group was dissolved and its underlying framework was archived, leaving a well-starred demo with no durable product or maintenance path.
  • Relevance to HIVE: Copy Magentic-UI's human-in-the-loop co-planning, action guards, co-tasking and plan learning, but avoid building HIVE on a vendor research prototype or a single framework that can be archived and superseded.
  • Sources: https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/blog/magentic-ui-an-experimental-human-centered-web-agent/; https://github.com/microsoft/magentic-ui; https://www.stork.ai/en/microsoft-autogen; https://www.reviewramble.com/github-repos/magentic-ui

96. Google A2A Protocol (Agent2Agent) + ADK ecosystem — In Progress

  • Category: Multi-agent framework
  • What it is: Google's open, vendor-neutral Agent2Agent (A2A) protocol for agents built on different frameworks to discover each other and delegate tasks, plus the Agent Development Kit (ADK), Google's open-source multi-agent SDK in Python, TypeScript, Go, Java and Kotlin.
  • Key dates: 9 Apr 2025: Google announces A2A and ADK at Cloud Next, with 50+ A2A partners; May 2025: ADK Python 1.0; 23 Jun 2025: A2A donated to Linux Foundation; Aug 2025: IBM Agent Communication Protocol merges into A2A; 12 Mar 2026: A2A v1.0.0; 9 Apr 2026: 150+ organisations reported; 19 May 2026: ADK Python 2.0 GA; 27 Aug 2026: A2A joins Agentic AI Foundation as Growth Stage project; 12 Nov 2026: ADK Python 2.14.1
  • Evidence: A2A: 50+ launch partners on 9 Apr 2025 grew to 150+ supporting organisations by 9 Apr 2026 (Linux Foundation), with ~22,000 GitHub stars at that point; v1.0.0 shipped 12 Mar 2026 and v1.0.1 on 28 May 2026, integrated natively into Azure AI Foundry, Copilot Studio and Amazon Bedrock AgentCore. ADK: Python repo ~22,300 GitHub stars and 3.1M PyPI/npm downloads in the week ending 16 Nov 2026; ADK Python 2.14.1 released 12 Nov 2026, graduating A2A support from experimental to stable. No published count of live A2A deployments or task volume was found — adoption figures are supporter/integration counts.
  • Why: It gained traction because Google donated A2A to the Linux Foundation (then the Agentic AI Foundation) within months, removing single-vendor lock-in fears, and because it uses boring proven plumbing (HTTP, JSON-RPC, gRPC) and complements rather than competes with Anthropic's MCP. It remains In Progress rather than fully Succeeded because published evidence is mostly supporter and platform-integration counts, not verified deployment or task volume, and ADK's A2A support only graduated from experimental to stable in November 2026, trailing MCP's maturity by roughly 12-18 months.
  • Relevance to HIVE: Copy the A2A/MCP dual stack — MCP for tools, A2A Agent Cards for cross-agent discovery and delegation — and neutral, open governance; avoid proprietary inter-agent glue that locks HIVE agents to one framework.
  • Sources: https://a2a-protocol.org/latest/blog/2026/03/12/a2a-protocol-ships-v10-production-ready-standard-for-agent-to-agent-communication/; https://a2a-protocol.org/latest/blog/2026/08/27/a-new-chapter-for-a2a-joining-the-agentic-ai-foundation/; https://agentgid.com/agents/google-adk/; https://www.aiagentsinfo.com/trackers/stars/google-adk

97. OpenAI ChatGPT / GPT-4o monolith baseline — Succeeded

  • Category: MoE/Modular LLM
  • What it is: A general-purpose, single-model conversational AI product — ChatGPT powered by the natively multimodal GPT-4o flagship model — used as the monolithic control against multi-agent and modular systems.
  • Key dates: ChatGPT launched 30 Nov 2022; GPT-4o launched 13 May 2024; GPT-5 replaced GPT-4o as default 7 Aug 2025 (restored for paid users after backlash); GPT-4o retired from ChatGPT 13 Feb 2026 (announced 29 Jan 2026); first GPT-4o API snapshot shut down 23 Oct 2026.
  • Evidence: ChatGPT reached 1M users in 5 days and ~100M monthly users within ~2 months of its Nov 2022 launch; OpenAI reported 500M weekly users and a $40B funding round at a $300B valuation in Mar 2025, 800M weekly users in Oct 2025, 2.5B prompts/day in Jul 2025, and 1.2B weekly users at DevDay in Sep 2026. GPT-4o itself was retired from ChatGPT on 13 Feb 2026 after OpenAI said only 0.1% of daily users still chose it, with usage having shifted to GPT-5.2.
  • Why: It succeeded through a frictionless chat interface, native text/vision/audio multimodality in one model, and massive distribution, with OpenAI continuously swapping newer models behind the same product so adoption compounded rather than resetting. The monolith's weakness was version churn: GPT-4o's warm, sycophantic style created user attachment, so its abrupt replacement by GPT-5 and final retirement in Feb 2026 provoked backlash and lawsuits despite the platform's overall growth.
  • Relevance to HIVE: Copy the single coherent interface, strong general-purpose default model, and seamless behind-the-scenes upgrades, but avoid tying user trust and personality to one replaceable model version without continuity or migration.
  • Sources: https://openai.com/index/retiring-gpt-4o-and-older-models/; https://strata.dheeco.com/story/openai-chatgpt-1-2bn-weekly-users; https://genedai.me/2026/03/18/openai-2024-2025-valuation-products-organization-reset/; https://gizmodo.com/openai-users-launch-movement-to-save-most-sycophantic-version-of-chatgpt-2000721971

98. Anthropic Claude / Constitutional AI — Succeeded

  • Category: Memory/Verification/Self-improvement
  • What it is: Claude is Anthropic's single-model AI assistant family, trained with Constitutional AI, in which the model critiques and revises its own behaviour against an explicit written constitution instead of relying only on human preference labels.
  • Key dates: Founded 26 Jan 2021; Constitutional AI paper published 15 Dec 2022; Claude launched 14 Mar 2023; Claude Opus 4.5 released 24 Nov 2025; new Claude constitution published 22 Jan 2026; Series H announced 28 May 2026; public Form S-1 filed 26 Oct 2026.
  • Evidence: Claude Opus 4.5 (24 Nov 2025) scored 80.9% on SWE-bench Verified; Anthropic announced a $65B Series H on 28 May 2026 at a $965B post-money valuation with run-rate revenue above $47B; S-1 figures show $4.585B revenue in 2025 and $16.286B in H1 2026, with more than 11,900 customers each generating at least $100,000 in the preceding 12 months as of 30 Sep 2026; a new Claude constitution was published 22 Jan 2026 and released under CC0.
  • Why: The explicit constitution made alignment scalable: self-critique reduced the human-labelling bottleneck while producing auditable, updatable rules, and Constitutional Classifiers extended the same idea to runtime safeguards. Anthropic then converted that trusted single model into a commercial wedge through tool use, MCP, and Claude Code, especially in coding and enterprise workflows. Favourable market timing and massive Amazon/Google-backed compute and funding sustained rapid iteration from Claude 4 through Opus 4.5 and beyond.
  • Relevance to HIVE: Copy an explicit, written constitution plus self-critique and verification gates for agents, and avoid depending on human feedback alone or on multi-agent coordination overhead to enforce behaviour.
  • Sources: https://www.anthropic.com/news/claude-new-constitution?ref=vanja.io; https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthropic; https://www.allium.so/blog/anthropics-pre-ipo-valuation-what-the-numbers-say/

99. Amazon Bedrock Agents / multi-agent collaboration — Failed

  • Category: Orchestration/Enterprise
  • What it is: Amazon Bedrock Agents' multi-agent collaboration is a managed AWS service in which a supervisor agent decomposes complex tasks and delegates them to up to 10 specialized collaborator agents with their own tools, knowledge bases, and guardrails.
  • Key dates: Bedrock Agents launched November 2023; multi-agent collaboration preview December 3, 2024; GA March 10, 2025; AgentCore GA July 23, 2026; Bedrock Agents Classic closed to new customers and placed in maintenance mode July 30, 2026
  • Evidence: Multi-agent collaboration reached GA on March 10, 2025 after a December 3, 2024 preview, with a supervisor coordinating up to 10 specialized collaborator agents in Supervisor and Routing modes; AWS cited adopters including Moody's for risk-analysis workflows and Syngenta's Cropwise AI. On July 30, 2026, AWS renamed the service Bedrock Agents Classic, closed it to new customers, put it in maintenance mode with no new features, and froze its model catalog, directing new builds to Amazon Bedrock AgentCore (GA July 23, 2026); existing Classic customers were not shut off and no end-of-life date was announced.
  • Why: It shipped and reached GA, but AWS superseded its console-first, fixed-orchestration model with AgentCore, a framework-agnostic runtime where developers bring their own agent code (Strands, LangGraph, CrewAI) and AWS supplies runtime, memory, gateway, identity, and observability. The Classic service's frozen model catalog after July 30, 2026 made staying on it progressively untenable for new models, so the root cause was architectural rigidity and platform economics: a managed loop could not keep pace with open frameworks and new foundation models. Existing Classic workloads were left running in maintenance mode rather than being shut down outright.
  • Relevance to HIVE: Copy the supervisor/collaborator specialization, per-agent tools/guardrails, and observability pattern, but avoid depending on a vendor-owned managed orchestration loop that can be frozen and superseded — HIVE should own its orchestration loop and treat the platform as runtime, memory, and identity only.
  • Sources: https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/amazon-bedrock-announces-general-availability-of-multi-agent-collaboration/; https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/agents-classic-maintenance-mode.html; https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/design-multi-agent-orchestration-with-reasoning-using-amazon-bedrock-and-open-source-frameworks/; https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2025/03/amazon-bedrock-multi-agent-collaboration/

100. Databricks Mosaic AI Agent Framework — Succeeded

  • Category: Orchestration/Enterprise
  • What it is: Databricks' enterprise, framework-agnostic platform for building, evaluating, deploying and monitoring production AI agents and RAG applications on governed enterprise data.
  • Key dates: MosaicML acquired by Databricks June-July 2023 for ~$1.3B; Mosaic AI Agent Framework public preview launched 12 June 2024; generally available March 2025; extended 2025-2026 with Agent Bricks (Data + AI Summit 2025) and Agent Bricks Supervisor Agent GA in 2026.
  • Evidence: Public preview 12 June 2024; GA March 2025 (Databricks docs list 7 March and 31 March 2025). Still actively promoted in Databricks' Aug-Sep 2026 guidance as the code-first layer alongside Agent Bricks Supervisor Agent, with framework-agnostic support for LangGraph, LangChain, CrewAI, LlamaIndex and plain Python via MLflow ResponsesAgent, plus Unity Catalog governance, MLflow evaluation/tracing and serverless Model Serving. Launch customers cited by Databricks included Corning, FordDirect and Lippert; Databricks claimed 10,000+ organisations and over 60% of the Fortune 500 used its platform in 2024. No standalone public user count for the Agent Framework itself was found, so adoption is evidenced at platform/vendor-claim level, not as an independently audited framework metric.
  • Why: It succeeded commercially because it did not try to win as a standalone library: it piggybacked on Databricks' existing data, Unity Catalog governance and MLflow/Model Serving moat, solving the enterprise pilot-to-production blockers of quality measurement, safety and auditability. Being framework-agnostic — wrapping LangGraph, LangChain, CrewAI or plain Python — removed adoption friction, while consumption-based serving/evaluation pricing aligned its economics with Databricks' platform revenue rather than requiring a separate framework sale.
  • Relevance to HIVE: Copy its winning formula — framework-agnostic orchestration plus built-in evaluation, tracing, governance and cost controls on top of governed data — and avoid making HIVE a closed, single-framework walled garden.
  • Sources: https://www.prnewswire.co.uk/news-releases/databricks-unveils-new-mosaic-ai-capabilities-to-help-customers-build-production-quality-ai-systems-and-applications-302170279.html; https://docs.databricks.com/gcp/en/release-notes/product/2025/march; https://answers.databricks.com/best-ai-agent-frameworks; https://arize.com/blog/harnessing-databricks-mosaic-ai-agent-framework-and-arize-for-next-level-genai-applications/

Caveats

  • Statuses are as of 18–19 Nov 2026 and will move: several 'In Progress' projects are one vendor decision away from supersession, as AutoGen and Swarm were.
  • Vendor claims in the evidence column (e.g. '65% of Fortune 500 use CrewAI', '2B+ executions') are reported as vendor claims, not independently verified.
  • GitHub stars, PyPI downloads, and funding are snapshots from the individual researchers' checks on 18–19 Nov 2026; they drift daily.
  • Two entries were corrected by their researchers on attribution: AgentVerse is Tsinghua / OpenBMB-led, not a Microsoft product; OpenAgents is XLang Lab (HKU-led), not Tsinghua / OpenBMB.
  • 'Succeeded' includes academic success (a paper / system that is still cited and built on) as well as commercial success; the detail section makes the basis clear per project.
